Read DSL.md, mcbuild/techniques.py, mcbuild/dsl.py, mcbuild/litematic.py, mcbuild/blockstate.py (rotate_state), mcbuild/ascii.py. Goal: make the window and pillar helpers reproduce the REAL reference cathedral instead of a hand-written approximation. The cathedral is refs/cathedral.litematic (load with `mcbuild.litematic.load_voxels`; coords x east, y up, z south; the north exterior wall of the nave runs along x with the wall plane at z=21, relief layers OUTWARD at z=20 and z=19 (north = -z = outward), interior at z=22). One window bay spans x=50..60, y=0..25. Look at it yourself first with:\n```\npython - <<EOF\nfrom mcbuild.litematic import load_voxels; from mcbuild.ascii import elevation\ncat = load_voxels(\"refs/cathedral.litematic\")\nfor z in (19,20,21,22):\n    sub={p:b for p,b in cat.items() if 50<=p[0]<=60 and 0<=p[1]<=25 and p[2]==z}\n    print(\"z\",z); print(elevation(sub,\"n\",bounds=(50,60,0,25,z,z)))\nEOF\n```\nThe window is a tracery lattice (walls, stairs, slabs, fences, trapdoors), the arch outline is made of stairs, and rows 4..9 repeat with a vertical period of 3.\n\nImplement, in a new module mcbuild/stamps.py plus small hooks in techniques.py/dsl.py:\n1. `extract_stamp(vox, x0,y0,z0,x1,y1,z1, face=\"n\") -> Stamp` — cut a box from a voxel dict into a local frame: u along the face (left→right seen from outside), v up, d outward (for face n: d = z_wall - z, so z=21 → d=0, z=20 → d=1, z=19 → d=2, z=22 → d=-1). Store as dict[(u,v,d)] -> block string (rotate_state applied so the stamp is expressed for face \"n\"). `Stamp` has width, height, depth range, and a `place(frame, u, v) -> Voxels` that maps (u,v,d) through `Frame.world` and rotates block states with `rotate_state(block, frame.turns)`.\n2. Build a catalog at import time (cache to catalog/cathedral_stamps.json so the litematic is not parsed at runtime; regenerate with `python -m mcbuild.stamps --rebuild`): \n   - `window_bay`: box x=50..60, y=0..25, z=19..22 (wall plane z=21). Record `wall_plane_d=0`, `sill_rows = 0..3`, `body_period = rows 4..9` (period 3, i.e. rows 4..6 repeat), `arch_rows = 10..25` (everything from the springing to the apex), `center_u = 5`.\n   - `pier`: the freestanding/attached pier column at x=60..62, y=0..28, z=19..21 (verify by inspection that this is the 3x3 ring pier; if the ring is elsewhere in that bay, pick the correct 3-wide column) including the cap/pinnacle rows at the top (y 25..28).\n   - `cornice`: the wall-top band at y=24..25 across x=50..60, z=19..22.\n3. `window_frame(..., style=\"cathedral\")`: structured crop/tiling of `window_bay` to the requested `width` (odd, 3..11) and `height` (>= 8): keep the `center_u` column and symmetric columns around it (crop columns from both edges equally); rows: take the sill rows, then repeat the body period until the remaining height minus the arch height is filled, then the arch rows cropped to the kept columns (when cropping columns, the arch outline must still close: recompute by keeping, for each arch row, only the kept columns — if the row becomes empty of outline, drop it; the apex block is at center_u). Opening blocks in the stamp that are air stay air through the wall (delete d=0..-(thickness-1)). Place outward layers at d=1,2 and inward at d=-1 when `thickness>=2` else skip d=-1. Provide `style=\"cathedral\"` as the DEFAULT for `Build.window`/`Build.windows` and keep the old parametric one as `style=\"pointed\"`.\n4. `corner_pillar(..., style=\"cathedral\")` default: tile the `pier` stamp vertically (its ring rows repeat; keep the cap/pinnacle rows at the top) to the requested height; fall back to the old code for size != 3.\n5. `eave(..., style=\"cathedral\")` default: tile the `cornice` stamp along the frame width (repeat its 11-wide period, crop at the ends symmetrically).\n6. Tests (tests/test_stamps.py): stamp extraction round-trips (placing the window_bay stamp back onto a Frame facing north at the original position reproduces the original cathedral voxels exactly for that box); rotation: placing on an east-facing frame then rotating back gives the same blocks; cathedral window at width 5 height 10 has: air opening columns, at least one fence (mullion) in the center column, stair blocks in the arch rows, and its total height == requested height; all four faces produce the same block multiset.\n7. {"file_path": "C:\\Users\\user\\mcbuild\\README.md", "content": "# mcbuild — a Minecraft building agent that builds like a master builder\n\nAI Tinkerers Global Hackathon, 2026-09-12. Theme: *Agents, Everywhere: Beyond the Chatbox*.\n\nA player types `!build a small gothic church ...` in Minecraft chat. GPT-6 Astra designs the\nbuilding, a harness made of the builder's own principles and the builder's own cathedral turns that\ndesign into block-level detail, and the building rises in the live world over RCON. The player then\nwalks around it and says `!fix make this tower taller` while looking at the tower; only the changed\nblocks are replaced, in place, while they watch.\n\n## What is new here\n\nLLMs can already lay out 3D massing. What they cannot do is place blocks the way an experienced\nMinecraft builder does. mcbuild puts a **harness** between the model and the world:\n\n1. **Spoken harness** — `mcbuild/prompts/AGENTS.md`: the builder's principles (odd widths and one\n   axis, windows with depth, pillars as the skeleton, stairs-only roofs, no flat walls, a foundation\n   scaled to the building). Written by the builder in Japanese, translated to English.\n2. **Coded harness** — `mcbuild/dsl.py` + `mcbuild/techniques.py`: small helper functions\n   (`window`, `corner_pillar`, `eave`, `gable_roof`, `buttress`, `plinth`, `beam`, `spire`, ...)\n   whose bodies encode the placements read from the builder's reference cathedral\n   (`refs/cathedral.litematic`, 186 x 109 x 81, 64k blocks, by the builder). The LLM never writes block\n   states or palette variants; it composes these helpers.\n\nAstra writes a `build.py` that calls the DSL. A fix request is a code edit; the world update is a\nvoxel diff.\n\n## Before / after (same photos, same prompt, harness added)\n\n| | plain Astra | Astra + harness |\n|---|---|---|\n| prompt | `experiments/plain_astra/prompt_v1.md` | `experiments/harness_astra/prompt_v2.md` (v1 + \"read AGENTS.md and DSL_REFERENCE.md, write with the DSL\") |\n| output | `experiments/plain_astra/church_plain.py` (raw voxel dict) | `experiments/harness_astra/build.py` (DSL script) |\n| blocks | 9,294 | 10,095 |\n| width | 30 | 29 (odd, per the builder's rule) |\n\nSubject: the Old Catholic Shimizu Church (Shizuoka, Japan, wooden Gothic, 1935, demolished 2024)\nat 2:1 scale, from six photos.\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\nMinecraft chat  --ssh tail-->  bridge.py  --codex exec (GPT-6 Astra)-->  edits builds/<id>/build.py\n                                    |                                          |\n                                    |<-- preview.py (ASCII elevations, check()) <-+\n                                    v\n                             apply.py: diff old/new voxels -> fill/setblock over RCON (rcon_batch.py on the server)\n```\n\n- `mcbuild/bridge.py` — tails the Paper server log, parses `!build` / `!fix` / `!undo` / `!status` /\n  `!cancel`, gathers player context (position, facing, the element the player is looking at via a\n  voxel raycast against the build's provenance map), runs `codex exec -m gpt-6-astra` non-interactively\n  (ChatGPT Plus login, no API key), then previews and applies.\n- `mcbuild/dsl.py`, `techniques.py`, `palette.py`, `blockstate.py` — the coded harness. Every element\n  call is tagged, so \"this window\" resolves to an element id like `nave_w_lancet_21`.\n- `mcbuild/apply.py`, `emit.py`, `runner.py`, `rcon_batch.py` — realization: run-length `fill`\n  compression, `/clone` backup for undo, forceload handling, streamed at a chosen commands/second so\n  the building visibly rises.\n- `mcbuild/player.py` — `data get entity` pose, view vector, Amanatides–Woo raycast, ground target.\n- `mcbuild/litematic.py`, `ascii.py` — reading the reference schematic, ASCII elevations used both by\n  humans and by Astra to check its own work.\n\n## Running it\n\nServer: Paper 1.21.8, flat world with ground at y=0, RCON enabled, creative. Fill `mcbuild/server.toml`.\n\n```\npip install nbtlib numpy pytest ruff\npython -m pytest -q                      # 107+ tests, no server needed\npython -m mcbuild.runner --deploy        # copy rcon_batch.py to the server box\npython -m mcbuild.preview tests/fixtures/chapel.py --faces n,e\npython -m mcbuild.bridge --build-id church --rate 60\n```\nThen in game: `!build a small gothic church`, `!fix make the spires taller`, `!undo`.\n\n## Honest notes\n\n- The reference cathedral schematic is a pre-existing asset by the builder. All code was written on\n  hackathon day (Claude Code with Sonnet/Fable agents implementing against `DSL.md` / `CONTRACT.md`).\n- The coded harness encodes the cathedral's grammar (pointed-arch stepping, relief frames, ringed\n  piers with bands and pinnacles, inverted-stair eaves, ridge ornaments). Work in progress:\n  stamping the cathedral's actual window tracery instead of a parametric approximation\n  (`mcbuild/stamps.py`).\n- Astra turn time is 1–4 minutes per request; the world update takes seconds.\n- Related work: Voyager / Mindcraft-style agents drive a bot; mcbuild is server-side (no bot),\n  and its contribution is the harness layer, not the agent loop.\n\n## Credits\n\nBuilder and principles: user (the builder). Design LLM: GPT-6 Astra via Codex CLI.\nImplementation: Claude Code.\n"}

説明文を `C:\Users\user\mcbuild\SUBMISSION.md` に書き、push 済みです（3e7645a）。中身はそのままコピーできます。

**タイトル**
mcbuild — an in-game Minecraft building agent that builds like a master builder

**説明（ポータル用、そのまま貼れる）**

Type `!build a small gothic church` in Minecraft chat and a church rises in the live world while you watch. Walk up to a tower, look at it, and say `!fix make this tower taller`: only the changed blocks are replaced, in place. No bot, no chatbox: the agent lives in the game server and in the builder's own workflow.

The LLM (GPT-6 Astra via Codex CLI) is good at 3D massing but places blocks like a beginner. mcbuild puts a harness between the model and the world, made of two things a master builder gave us:

1. **His principles as the system prompt** — odd widths and one axis, windows with real depth, pillars as the skeleton, stairs-only roofs, no flat walls, a foundation scaled to the building.
2. **His cathedral as code** — small helper functions (`window`, `corner_pillar`, `eave`, `gable_roof`, `buttress`, `plinth`, `beam`, `spire`) whose bodies encode the placements read from his 64,000-block reference cathedral, including a window style stamped directly from the schematic's tracery.

Astra composes these helpers into a build script; the harness resolves block states, palette variants and detail. A fix request is a code edit, the world update is a voxel diff, and every element carries a name so "this window" means the window you are looking at (position, view direction and a raycast against the build are passed to the model each turn).

Before/after in the video: the same six photos of the Old Catholic Shimizu Church (Shizuoka, 1935) and the same prompt, once with plain Astra and once with the harness. Then three live `!fix` turns: roof raised, towers and spires raised, windows re-centred. Each turn takes 2–4 minutes of model time and 6–50 seconds to land in the world.

Stack: Paper 1.21.8 server + RCON, Python harness (DSL, techniques, voxel diff, `/clone` undo), Codex CLI non-interactive with a ChatGPT Plus login (no API key), Claude Code for implementation. All code was written on hackathon day; the reference cathedral schematic and the builder's principles are the builder's prior work.

Repo: https://github.com/example-user/minecraft-building-agent

**SNS 投稿**

Built for #AgentsEverywhere at the @aitinkerers Global Hackathon: a Minecraft building agent that builds like a master builder. Chat in game, GPT-6 Astra designs, a harness made of the builder's principles + his cathedral's block placements does the detail, and fixes land live as voxel diffs. Repo: https://github.com/example-user/minecraft-building-agent

パートナーのハンドルは提出フォームの記載に合わせて足してください。動画に映した `!fix` の回数や内容が説明文の「3 回」と違うなら、その一文だけ直してください。

--- [text] ---
mcbuild is a building agent that lives inside a Minecraft server. Players talk to it in the game's own chat, and it answers with buildings: type `!build a small gothic church` and the church rises in the live world in front of you; walk up to a tower, look at it, and type `!fix make this tower taller`, and only the changed blocks are replaced, in place, while you watch. There is no bot avatar and no separate chat window. The agent is present where the players already are, and it uses what that environment gives it: your position, the direction you are looking, and the exact building element under your crosshair.

The problem. Large language models can lay out a building's massing, but they place blocks like a beginner: flat walls, glass-in-a-hole windows, roofs with gaps, no rhythm. Experienced Minecraft builders have craft knowledge (how a pointed arch steps, how a pier is ringed, how an eave overhangs) that never reaches the model. mcbuild puts a harness between the model and the world, made of two things one master builder gave us. First, his principles as the system prompt: odd widths and a single axis, windows with real depth, pillars as the skeleton, stairs-only roofs, no flat walls, a foundation scaled to the building. Second, his cathedral as code: small helper functions (`window`, `corner_pillar`, `eave`, `gable_roof`, `buttress`, `plinth`, `beam`, `spire`) whose bodies encode the block placements read from his 64,000-block reference cathedral schematic, including a window style stamped directly from the schematic's tracery and cropped to any size. The design model (GPT-6 Astra) composes these helpers into a build script; the harness resolves block states, palette variants and detail. We validated the idea with a controlled comparison: the same six photos of the Old Catholic Shimizu Church and the same prompt, once with plain Astra and once with the harness. The harness version has the builder's proportions, relief and rhythm; the plain version is a recognisable but flat shell.

Why the environment matters. This workflow cannot be reproduced in a chatbox. The agent reads the player's pose from the server, casts a ray against its own build to find the element being looked at, and passes "you are looking at nave_w_lancet_21 on the west wall" to the model, so "this window" means this window. A fix is a code edit to the build script, and the world update is a voxel diff, so the player sees exactly what changed and nothing else, streamed at a chosen rate so the change rises visibly. The building is a shared, persistent artifact in a world other players walk through; the agent's output is the place itself. Judged as a chatbot it would be a paragraph of block coordinates; judged in the world it is a church you can walk into and correct by pointing at it.

Technical execution. The server is Paper 1.21.8 with RCON on a flat creative world. The harness is Python 3.12: a litematic reader (nbtlib, numpy) that extracts the reference cathedral; a builder DSL with provenance so every voxel knows which named element produced it; a palette system that maps material families to stairs, slabs, walls, fences and trapdoors; explicit block-state resolution (stair facing and corner shapes, fence and wall connections, log axes) rather than relying on server updates; ASCII elevation rendering used both by humans and by the model to check its own work before it finishes a turn; a `check()` pass for floating blocks, unsupported stairs, overlapping elements and the builder's odd-width rule. Realization compresses runs into `fill` commands, backs up the site with `/clone` so `!undo` restores the original terrain, force-loads chunks, and streams commands through a small RCON client running on the server box over ssh so a 10,000-block church lands in about 90 seconds and a typical fix in 6–50 seconds. The chat bridge tails the server log over ssh, parses `!build` / `!fix` / `!undo` / `!status` / `!cancel`, runs Codex CLI non-interactively with GPT-6 Astra under a ChatGPT Plus login (no API key), serialises turns so two fixes can never clobber the same script, and reports results back into game chat. Turns are logged as JSONL, and each turn's patch can be reverted individually, which we used live when one change was not wanted. 113 tests cover the DSL, block states, run-length emission, RCON framing against a fake server that mimics Minecraft's one-packet-per-read quirk, the raycast, the diff and undo logic, and the chat parser with real Paper log samples. Failure handling includes UTF-8 decoding of Japanese chat, a preview gate before any world write, atomic state saves only on fully successful applies, and a crash we diagnosed on the day (Paper's bundled profiler on Java 26) documented in the repo.

Usefulness and experience. For a builder, the agent turns hours of block-by-block work into a conversation held inside the world: describe, look, correct. Each request is answered in game chat with a one-line summary and a block count, the player keeps full control through `!undo`, `!cancel` and per-turn revert, and the harness is transparent: the principles are a readable document and the techniques are readable functions, so the builder can change how the agent builds by editing his own text. In our live session the loop ran end to end four times on two buildings, with model time of 2–4 minutes per request and world updates of a few seconds. The pattern generalises to any server with a house style: encode the style once as prompt plus helpers, and every player gets an agent that builds the way the server's best builder does.

Repository: https://github.com/example-user/minecraft-building-agent

GitHub の中身だけでは実機ループは動きません。動く範囲と足りないものを README に書き、実際に新規クローンで確かめました。

**クローンだけで動くもの（確認済み）**

- ハーネス本体（DSL・部品関数・大聖堂の窓 stamp）、preview、差分適用・ブリッジのコード、テスト。
- 新規クローンで `pytest` が 111 件通過・2 件スキップ、Astra が書いた教会の preview が警告 0。
- そのために `catalog/cathedral_stamps.json`（大聖堂の窓 1 ベイ分、11×26×4 ブロックの抜粋、36 KB）を公開に含めました。大聖堂全体の litematic は非公開のままです。抜粋も出したくなければ言ってください。外して、代わりに「schematic を `refs/` に置いて `python -m mcbuild.stamps --rebuild`」の手順にします。

**クローンに無くて、実機ループに要るもの（README の「Running it from a clean clone」に記載）**

1. Minecraft サーバー: Paper 1.21.8、平地（地面 y=0）、RCON 有効、ssh と python3 が使える機体。設定の要件書は `docs/server_requirements.md`（ホスト名やプレイヤー名は伏せ字にした版）。`mcbuild/server.toml` に接続先を書く。
2. Codex CLI 0.153 以上で ChatGPT にログイン済みで、`gpt-6-astra` が使えること。API キーは不要。Astra が自己確認に使う Python のパスは `turn_prefix.txt` で各自の環境に合わせる。
3. チャットを打つプレイヤーのアカウント（ホワイトリスト登録）。

比較実験の再現には清水教会の写真（`refs/shimizu/`）も要ります。これも README に書きました。

リポジトリは 2 コミット（6ab2aee、0a12c15）で、個人情報の再スキャンも異常なしです。
